## Session 68: Review of the Visibility Prototype (Session 67); Artist Pipeline Guide

- **Date:** 20261021
- **Developer:** uraymeiviar
- **Agent:** Claude Code (Opus 5.5), review of Session 67
- **Branch:** main
- **Base Commit SHA:** dbbf1fd

### Review of Session 67

- **Verified by the reviewer:**
  - the synthetic validation passes (front versus back, behind a shell, half covered 0.51, recessed niche 0.18 against 0.74);
  - GPU face-ID rendering in background Blender works and is fast (45 s for the full cockpit, as reported);
  - the cube map's "forward" face looks along -Y, which is the cockpit's forward direction (all cockpit cameras look along -Y).
- **The cockpit result is not usable yet.** `pilot_demand.png` is uniformly saturated blue, not "panels blue and neutral grey" as Session 67 describes it; nearly every visible face is above the colour scale (the overall median is 2.27 times the reference).

### Findings

1. **Views measured at different pixel densities are mixed.** The look-around cube map renders 1,024 px over 90 degrees (about 650 px per radian); the fixed view renders 2,560 × 1,440 at the camera's 51.9 degree vertical field (about 1,590 px per radian). Pixels per square metre from the fixed view are therefore about 6 times those of the cube map, and the per-face maximum takes the fixed view almost everywhere it sees. Session 10's design uses one screen reference for the whole analysis; demands must be normalised to it (for example pixels per steradian at the reference resolution and field of view).
2. **Cube faces are not summed:** a face seen across two cube faces keeps the larger of the two counts instead of their sum, so large panels at cube edges are undercounted.
3. **Animation passes are scene frames 0, 50 and 100,** not 0, 50 and 100 % of each action's own length (decision 21). In the E2 cockpit most of the 85 actions run 0 to 100, which matches, but others run 100 to 200, 50 to 100, 0 to 101 or 1 to 100.
4. **Invisible and transparent materials block the view.** `WINDSHIELD_COLLISION` (invisible `COLLIDER` material, 4 faces around the cockpit) is rendered as a solid occluder and scored itself (the box in `outside_demand.png`). Invisible, collision and occluder materials must neither occlude nor be scored; decal, glass and windshield materials must not occlude what lies behind them (a decal on a panel would otherwise make the panel look hidden).
5. **Never-seen objects are not delete candidates as such:** the 334 listed include switch states and similar objects (for example `OVHD_APU_MASTER_ON`) that the model shows only under conditions the analysis does not evaluate. The report must say "never seen in the analysed states", for the artist to judge.
6. **The session text is damaged:** escape sequences were swallowed when it was written (`\t` in "tests", `\b` in "blender", `\n` before "np.bincount", part of the `62.5^2` expression). The content is readable; the session is left as written (append-only).
7. **Decisions 67 to 71 are recorded as the maintainer's.** To be confirmed by the maintainer, in particular decision 69 ("travel" percentages), which the code implements as scene frames (Finding 3).

### Artist Pipeline Guide

`docs/user/README.md` now leads with **The Pipeline**: ten steps from a new model to a deployed one (scene and names; zones and materials; units and loose pieces; merge tree; LOD; seams and unwrap; visibility scoring; packing; texturing; export and deploy). Each step gives why MSFS needs it, what to do with which tool, how to check it, and for steps whose tools are in development (zones, visibility scoring, packing) what the tool will do and how to do it by hand meanwhile. It links to the reference pages and names the aircraft repositories' MSFS Authoring Guide (`doc/msfs-authoring/`) for the underlying rules.

Agreed with the maintainer for this guide:

- the same pipeline for cockpit and exterior (camera sets, LOD sphere and zones differ);
- written for artists who know Blender, about MSFS and our tools;
- step 3 (units) is a modelling decision about what disappears together at LOD, independent of the merge tree; `@MERGE_` sits on high levels (a floor or a panel), and tags with **Don't Merge** keep screens and interactive objects separate.

### Affected Files

- `docs/user/README.md`

## Session 69: Visibility Score Model, Density Formula, Hand-Off (Visibility Prototype Round 2)

- **Date:** 20261021
- **Developer:** uraymeiviar
- **Agent:** Claude Code (Opus 5.5)
- **Branch:** main
- **Base Commit SHA:** 9c7c0ec

### Maintainer Answers on Decisions 67 to 71

- **67, confirmed:** results in a separate `<blend name>.visibility` file next to the `.blend`, never in mesh data; deleting the file resets the analysis.
- **68, refined:** each Mirror copy (several with several modifiers) is scored like its own object; each face of the original keeps the maximum over its copies (as the prototype does).
- **69, corrected:** animation states are percentages of each animation's own length, not scene frames. Default 0, 50 and 100 %; an artist option for more states, for example five (0, 25, 50, 75, 100 %) for parts that turn a full revolution. Each face keeps its maximum over the states.
- **70, replaced** by decisions 72 to 75 below (no reference view defining 1.0).
- **71, kept:** maximum per face over cameras and states; an island's (or object's) value is the area-weighted 90th percentile of its faces.

### Maintainer Decisions (continued)

72. **The visibility score is a factor from 0 to 1, independent of distance.** For one camera and one animation state, a face's score is its **visible fraction** (the solid angle of the face actually seen from the camera position, divided by the solid angle the face would cover if nothing were in front of it) times the **direction weight** (decision 73). Distance appears in both parts of the fraction and cancels; occlusion is the eye's own (perspective from the camera position). No foreshortening term: an edge-on face that is not hidden counts as visible. The score of a face is the maximum over cameras and animation states (decision 71).
73. **Direction weight from free look:** MSFS lets the user pan around each camera's default view. The weight is 1 in the camera's default forward direction and falls off with the angle the head must turn: `w = max(floor, cos(angle))` as a starting point, floor 0.2 (an artist option, like the falloff). Zoom is not modelled for now.
74. **Never-seen faces** (score 0 in every analysed camera and state) get a minimum factor at density time (0.1 by default) and are reported as "never seen in the analysed views and states" for the artist to judge, not as delete candidates (switch states and condition-dependent parts are not seen in the analysed states).
75. **Texel density formula** (per length, pixels per centimetre):
    - **texel density ratio** = zone multiplier × object mark × visibility score, each 1.0 by default;
    - **texel density** = ratio × target texel density, between a lower and an upper bound that give the packer its freedom to scale an island down or up.

    The zone multiplier lets the artist set how much texture a zone gets (distance is the artist's choice here, not part of the score); the object mark is the per-object multiplier in UV Marks (a manual override, for example the glareshield, and the fallback while no visibility file exists); visibility fine-tunes per island.
76. **The zone multiplier is stored on the zone empty** (`@ZONE_<NAME>`, decision 28) and inherited by everything below it, like the other marks.
77. **Material classes in the visibility analysis:** invisible, collision and occluder materials neither hide anything nor are scored; decal, glass and windshield materials are scored but do not hide what lies behind them (a decal on a panel must not make the panel look hidden). The prototype rendered `WINDSHIELD_COLLISION` (invisible `COLLIDER`) as a solid wall (Session 68, Finding 4).

### Open (for the packing work)

- How the existing UV Marks density marks (presets Lower 0.7 to 0.8, Higher 1.5 to 1.65, default range 0.9 to 1.1) become the single object multiplier of decision 75, and where the target texel density and its bounds are set (per zone, from the zone material's texture size).
- The exterior camera set: orthographic views from around a sphere may suit "seen from outside, from afar"; decided when the exterior is analysed.

### Hand-Off: Visibility Prototype Round 2

**Read first:** `AGENTS.md`; this journal's Sessions 10, 67, 68 and 69; `tests/uv_prototype/visibility_proto.py` and its section in `tests/uv_prototype/RUN.md`.

**Inputs:** `G:\e-jets-e2\assets\visual\cockpit\COCKPIT.blend` (read-only; work on the copy in `tests/_work/uv_visibility/`, refresh the copy first). Cameras in the file: `CAM_PILOT`, `CAM_PILOT_CLOSE`, `CAM_PILOT_LANDING`, `CAM_COPILOT` (all look along -Y).

**Steps** (all changes in `tests/uv_prototype/visibility_proto.py`; this is still a prototype, not add-on code):

1. **Visible fraction (decision 72),** measured in solid angle, so cube-map and frustum views are comparable:
   - weight every rendered pixel by its solid angle (a per-pixel solid angle map for the cube-map faces and for the frustum), and sum the visible solid angle per face over **all views of a camera** (the six cube faces together; Session 68, Finding 2);
   - compute each face's unoccluded solid angle analytically from the camera position (the solid angle of its triangles, zero for faces turned away);
   - visible fraction = visible / unoccluded, clamped to 0..1.
2. **Direction weight (decision 73):** weight each pixel by `max(floor, cos(angle))` between its ray and the camera's default forward direction, before summing; floor and falloff as options. Keep the fixed-view mode (frustum only, weight 1 inside it) as an option per camera.
3. **Animation states as percentages (decision 69):** evaluate each animated object's action (and NLA strips) at the percentage of its own frame range, not at a scene frame; default three states, an option for five. State the method, and report actions or objects it cannot handle. Test on an action running 100 to 200.
4. **Material classes (decision 77):** leave invisible, collision and occluder materials out of rendering; render decal, glass and windshield after the opaque pass without writing depth, so they are scored but hide nothing. Use the material class name (decision 30) or the MSFS material type, as `uv_autoseam._is_skip_material` does.
5. **Mirror (decision 68):** keep the current mapping of mirrored copies to original faces with the maximum.
6. **Aggregation and report (decisions 71 and 74):** the per-face score is the maximum over cameras and states; per island (UV islands of the active UV map) and per object the area-weighted 90th percentile. Never-seen faces are reported as "never seen in the analysed views and states", with counts per object, not as delete candidates.
7. **`.visibility` file (decision 67):** a schema version, the cameras with their modes and options, the states, and per mesh the face scores; loading it back must reproduce the same summary.
8. **Images:** the score 0 to 1 on a colour scale (never seen in magenta, as the heatmap's Error colour) from the pilot camera and from outside; inspect and describe them.
9. **Synthetic validation** (new cases, keep the old ones where they still apply):
   - the same panel, fully visible, at 0.5 m and at 2 m: equal scores (distance-free);
   - a panel half covered: about 0.5;
   - a panel straight ahead against one 90 degrees to the side: weights 1 and about the floor;
   - a back face: never seen;
   - an invisible box between camera and panel: the panel stays fully visible;
   - a decal on a panel: the panel stays fully visible, the decal is scored;
   - an object animated on frames 100 to 200: evaluated at its own 0, 50 and 100 %.
10. **Cockpit run** on the copy with `CAM_PILOT_CLOSE` and `CAM_COPILOT` as look-around cameras: run time per state and camera, the score distribution (percentiles), never-seen faces and area, the top and bottom 20 objects, and a comparison with Session 67.
11. **Append Session 70** and commit only `tests/uv_prototype/visibility_proto.py`, `tests/uv_prototype/RUN.md` and this journal, by name, with the `Journal:` trailer. No push.

**Finish line:** all synthetic cases pass; the cockpit run finishes within 10 minutes for two cameras and three states; the pilot image shows a graded score, not one saturated colour. A criterion that is not met is reported as NOT MET with its numbers, never as passed with an explanation.

**Do not:** change `fss_blender_tools/`; write into `G:\e-jets-e2` or `F:\new-fss-aircraft-ejet`; start Blender while another Blender process is running; run Blender interactively; record maintainer decisions (open questions go into Session 70).
